# Architecture Example: a multi-package platform

## Multi-Package — GridLoad Platform (Monorepo)

**Context:** The GridLoad project has grown. The spec now covers meter and weather ingestion on a schedule, an as-of store, feature research, held-out forecast evaluation, a live day-ahead forecast service, and reporting. Parts run on different schedules (nightly ingestion, interactive research, a long-running day-ahead service), and the team wants research code unable to import the live service's code.

**Step 1 — Shape:** "Multi-package. Ingestion and the day-ahead service are deployed separately from the research library. Proposed packages and direction:"

```
reports → forecast → features → data → core
ingest → data
dispatch → features, data
```

The user approves the package map before any package doc is written.

**Result — root `docs/architecture.md` (abridged):**

````markdown
# GridLoad — Architecture

**Status:** Draft · **Shape:** Multi-package · **Last updated:** 2026-10-14 · **Spec:** [spec.md](spec.md)

## Overview
Nightly, `ingest` pulls the utility's meter export and the weather into an as-of store. Researchers use `data`, `features`, and `forecast` from notebooks. `dispatch` runs separately, building each morning's day-ahead forecast and reserve allocation. `reports` builds accuracy reports from held-out and live results.

## Packages
| Package | Path | Responsibility | Covers | Depends on | Doc |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `core` | packages/core | Shared types: hours, regions, calendars, units | QR-03 | — | [core](packages/core/architecture.md) |
| `data` | packages/data | Read-only as-of query API | DATA-01–DATA-06 | `core` | [data](packages/data/architecture.md) |
| `ingest` | packages/ingest | Scheduled download, validation, and write to the store | ING-01–ING-04 | `core`, `data` | [ingest](packages/ingest/architecture.md) |
| `features` | packages/features | Feature definitions and seasonal profiles | FEAT-01–FEAT-05 | `core`, `data` | [features](packages/features/architecture.md) |
| `forecast` | packages/forecast | Seasonal-naive and boosted forecasts, scored on held-out weeks | FC-01–FC-06 | `core`, `data`, `features` | [forecast](packages/forecast/architecture.md) |
| `dispatch` | packages/dispatch | Day-ahead forecast and reserve service | DSP-01–DSP-04 | `core`, `data`, `features` | [dispatch](packages/dispatch/architecture.md) |
| `reports` | packages/reports | Accuracy reports and comparisons | REP-01–REP-03 | `core`, `forecast` | [reports](packages/reports/architecture.md) |

## Dependency rules
- `core` imports nothing internal.
- Only `ingest` writes to the store; everything else reads through `data`.
- `features` and `forecast` never import `dispatch` or `ingest`.
- Enforced by `import-linter` contracts in CI (AD-03).

## Cross-package contracts
- `data.AsOfStore` — provided by `data`, used by every package. [Contract](packages/data/contract.md)
- `features.Features` (hour × region → feature values) — provided by `features`, used by `forecast` and `dispatch`. [Contract](packages/features/contract.md)
- `forecast.Result` (predictions, errors, baseline errors) — provided by `forecast`, used by `reports`. [Contract](packages/forecast/contract.md)

## System pipelines
### PL-01: Held-out evaluation
**Trigger:** called from a research notebook · **Covers:** FEAT-01–FEAT-05, FC-01–FC-06, REP-01
1. `data` → `features`: `data.AsOfStore` (readings, and the weather forecast as issued, at each forecast time)
2. `features` → `forecast`: `features.Features`
3. `forecast` → `reports`: `forecast.Result`

### PL-02: Day-ahead run
**Trigger:** `dispatch` service, each morning at 06:00 · **Covers:** DSP-01–DSP-04
1. `data` → `features`: `data.AsOfStore` (as of 06:00 that day)
2. `features` → `dispatch`: `features.Features`

*(PL-03, nightly ingest, omitted here.)*

## Project layout
```
packages/core/  packages/data/  packages/ingest/  packages/features/
packages/forecast/  packages/dispatch/  packages/reports/
```

## Shared tech stack and tooling
| Area | Choice | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| Workspace | uv workspace, one lockfile | AD-01 |
| Language | Python 3.12 | AD-02 |
| Store | Parquet files + DuckDB queries | AD-04 |
| Weather data | Provider B | AD-05 |

Packages are versioned together; one release tag for the repo.

## Decisions
### AD-05: Weather data provider
**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-14 · **Scope:** `ingest`, `data`
- **Context:** Checked against DATA-01 (hourly weather for every service region since 2015), DATA-03 (forecasts stamped with the hour they were issued, *not only actuals*), and the brief's budget (< $100/month).
- **Decision:** Provider B.
- **Alternatives:** Provider A (free) — fails DATA-03: actuals only, no archived forecasts. Provider C — meets everything but costs about 4× the budget.
- **Consequences:** Ingestion is written against Provider B's API behind `ingest.Source`, so a later switch touches one module.

*(AD-01–AD-04 omitted here.)*

## Changelog
| Date | Change | Source | IDs |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-10-14 | Initial architecture | spec.md | AD-01–AD-05 |
````

**Each package** gets a folder. `docs/packages/data/architecture.md` uses the single-package template for that package: its modules, its data layout and its folders, with Interfaces linking to `contract.md` beside it. It carries no Decisions or Changelog; those live in the root. `docs/packages/data/contract.md` holds `### data.AsOfStore: …` and the other public symbols, with signatures, invariants and errors. `pipeline-design` later adds each package's `pipelines.md` to the same folder.

Each cross-package symbol names its boundary, so `build-planning` can freeze it before either side is built. `docs/packages/features/contract.md`:

```markdown
### features.Features: type
`Features = DataFrame[FeatureSchema]  # index (hour, region), columns temp_forecast, lag_24h, lag_168h: float64`
- **Invariants:** one row per (hour, region) for regions with an active meter that hour; values finite; hours ascending
- **Errors:** constructing one from a frame that fails `FeatureSchema` raises `pandera.errors.SchemaError`
- **Boundary:** forecast, dispatch · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/features.Features/ · **Cases:** two days × three regions, a meter decommissioned mid-sample, a region with a flat-load day
- **Used by:** forecast, dispatch

### features.build_features: Hourly features
`def build_features(store: AsOfStore, start: datetime, end: datetime) -> Features`
- **Invariants:** each hour uses only weather forecasts issued before it (DATA-03)
- **Errors:** `ValueError` if `end` is not after `start`
- **Boundary:** forecast, dispatch · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/features.build_features/ · **Cases:** the two-day sample above
- **Used by:** forecast, dispatch
```

Both sides are Python, so the schema is a pandera schema and the fixtures are sample frames. Between runtimes that share no types (a TypeScript service and a Swift app), the schema would be JSON Schema and the fixtures JSON payloads. The contract names the cases; the milestone's interface task writes the schema and one fixture per case. `build_features` is a function other packages call, so its Boundary line gets a fake `features` too; a type needs only its samples.

**Notice:**
- The data provider decision is checked against specific `DATA-` requirements and the brief's budget. That's how "which API?" gets answered. The spec stayed vendor-neutral, and the decision lives here.
- The dependency rules are enforceable, not aspirational.
- The root doc stays a map. Detail lives in package docs, so each one stays a readable 1-3 pages.
- System pipelines say which package hands what to which, through which contract. The functions and files inside each package are left to `pipelines.md`.
- Each boundary's cases are named here, so `forecast` and `dispatch` can build against the frozen `Features` samples in the same milestone instead of waiting for `features`.

D85Write/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-9/quant-all-at-once/with_skill/project/docs/architecture.md (272 lines)

# Quant Research Platform — Architecture

**Status:** Draft · **Shape:** Multi-package · **Last updated:** 2026-10-17 · **Spec:** [spec.md](spec.md)

## Overview
`data` downloads Sharadar, FRED and SEC data into an immutable, bitemporal snapshot store, validates each snapshot and promotes it, and serves every later stage one point-in-time view. `lab` holds the shared walk-forward schedule, the hypothesis ledger, the backtest and the S2–S3 research (factors, denoising, covariance ranking, classical portfolios, features, significance), plus the final verdict. `models` holds the torch-based S4 work: forecasters, forecast-driven portfolios and PPO agents, trained on the laptop or on a rented GPU. `server` is a local-only HTTP API over `data` that the TypeScript `app` reads for the explorer and the live run monitor.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
  vendors[(Sharadar · FRED · SEC EDGAR)] --> data
  data --> lab
  data --> models
  lab --> models
  data --> server
  server -- HTTP + SSE, wire schemas --> app
  models -. training bundle .-> gpu[(rented GPU)]
```

## Packages
| Package | Path | Responsibility | Covers | Depends on | Doc |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `data` | packages/data | Download, store, validate and serve point-in-time daily data and run records | UNIV-01–UNIV-04, INGST-01–INGST-06, VALID-01–VALID-08, PANEL-01–PANEL-06, DATA-01–DATA-07, QR-01, QR-03 | — | [data](packages/data/architecture.md) |
| `server` | packages/server | Local HTTP + SSE API over `data`; owns the wire schemas | EXPL-01–EXPL-05 (backend), RUNS-01–RUNS-04 (backend), QR-04, QR-06, QR-08 | `data` | [server](packages/server/architecture.md) |
| `app` | packages/app | Browser explorer and live run monitor | EXPL-01–EXPL-06, RUNS-01–RUNS-04, QR-06, QR-08 | `server` (HTTP only) | [app](packages/app/architecture.md) |
| `lab` | packages/lab | Walk-forward schedule, ledger, backtest, S2–S3 research and the verdict | WFWD-01–WFWD-06, LEDG-01–LEDG-06, BTEST-01–BTEST-04, FACT-01–FACT-05, DENOI-01–DENOI-03, COV-01–COV-04, PORT-01–PORT-06, FEAT-01–FEAT-04, SIG-01–SIG-04, VERD-01–VERD-03, QR-07 | `data` | [lab](packages/lab/architecture.md) |
| `models` | packages/models | Neural forecasters, forecast-driven portfolios, PPO agents, GPU bundles | FCST-01–FCST-07, FPORT-01–FPORT-03, AGENT-01–AGENT-05, QR-02, QR-05 | `lab`, `data` | [models](packages/models/architecture.md) |

## Dependency rules
- `data` imports nothing internal. Only `data` writes the snapshot store or run records; everything else reads through its contract.
- `lab` may import `data`; never `models`, `server`.
- `models` may import `lab` and `data`; it is the only package that imports `torch`, `gymnasium` or `stable_baselines3`.
- `server` may import `data` only, and only `server` imports `fastapi`.
- `app` imports no Python; it talks to `server` over HTTP through the generated wire types.
- Enforced by `import-linter` contracts over the uv workspace and a forbidden-import check for the heavy stacks (AD-01).

## Cross-package contracts
- `data.membership_as_of`, `data.returns_panel`, `data.bars_panel`, `data.risk_free`, `data.sectors`, `data.Calendar` — provided by `data`, used by `lab`, `models`, `server`. [Contract](packages/data/contract.md)
- `data.stock_history`, `data.find_stock`, `data.coverage`, `data.issues`, `data.list_runs`, `data.run_events` — provided by `data`, used by `server`. [Contract](packages/data/contract.md)
- `server` wire types and routes (`server.MembershipBody` … `server.stream_run`) — provided by `server`, used by `app`. [Contract](packages/server/contract.md)
- `lab.Schedule`, `lab.training_window`, `lab.guard`, ledger (`lab.register` … `lab.entries`), `lab.backtest`, `lab.check_weights`, `lab.project_capped`, `lab.forward_targets`, `lab.compute_features`, `lab.surviving_features`, `lab.benchmark` — provided by `lab`, used by `models`. [Contract](packages/lab/contract.md)
- `models` publishes no symbol another package imports; its results reach `lab.write_verdict` only through ledger entries. [Contract](packages/models/contract.md)

## System pipelines
### PL-01: Data refresh
**Trigger:** `qrp data download --full|--update [--resume RUN]` from the command line · **Covers:** INGST-01–INGST-06, VALID-01–VALID-08, RUNS-01–RUNS-04, UNIV-04
1. `data` (ingest → store): `data.run_download` writes a staged `data.Snapshot` and run events
2. `data` (validate): `data.validate_snapshot` quarantines, promotes or rejects; `data.ValidationResult`
3. `data` → `server`: `data.list_runs`, `data.run_events` (polled from the run database)
4. `server` → `app`: `server.stream_run` (SSE of `server.RunEventBody`), `server.list_runs`

### PL-02: Explore the dataset
**Trigger:** the researcher opens the app at `http://127.0.0.1:<port>` · **Covers:** EXPL-01–EXPL-06, QR-08
1. `app` → `server`: `server.get_membership`, `server.search_stocks`, `server.get_stock`, `server.get_coverage`, `server.get_issues`
2. `server` → `data`: `data.membership_as_of`, `data.find_stock`, `data.stock_history`, `data.coverage`, `data.issues` on the current snapshot

### PL-03: Covariance estimator ranking
**Trigger:** `qrp lab rank-covariance --entry-family COV` · **Covers:** WFWD-01–WFWD-06, FACT-01–FACT-05, DENOI-01–DENOI-03, COV-01–COV-04, BTEST-01–BTEST-04, LEDG-01–LEDG-05
1. `data` → `lab`: `data.returns_panel`, `data.risk_free`, `data.membership_as_of` pinned to one snapshot
2. `lab` (walkforward → covariance → portfolios → backtest → ledger): `lab.Schedule`, `lab.CovMatrix`, `lab.Weights`, `lab.BacktestResult`, `lab.TestOutcome`

### PL-04: Classical portfolios and the S4 benchmark
**Trigger:** `qrp lab classical` after PL-03's ranking is recorded · **Covers:** PORT-01–PORT-06, BTEST-01–BTEST-04, LEDG-01–LEDG-05
1. `data` → `lab`: as PL-03 step 1
2. `lab` (portfolios → backtest → ledger): `lab.Weights`, `lab.BacktestResult`; stores `lab.benchmark`

### PL-05: Feature significance
**Trigger:** `qrp lab features` after PL-04 · **Covers:** FEAT-01–FEAT-04, SIG-01–SIG-04, FACT-05
1. `data` → `lab`: `data.bars_panel`, `data.returns_panel`
2. `lab` (factors → targets → features → significance → ledger): `lab.FeaturePanel`, `lab.Targets`, `lab.TestOutcome`

### PL-06: Forecaster training
**Trigger:** `qrp models forecast --config <file>` on the laptop; the fit step on the GPU host · **Covers:** FCST-01–FCST-07, QR-02, QR-05
1. `data` → `models`: `data.bars_panel`, `data.sectors`, `data.Calendar`
2. `lab` → `models`: `lab.training_window`, `lab.guard`, `lab.forward_targets`, `lab.compute_features`, `lab.surviving_features`
3. `models` → `lab`: ledger entries (`lab.register`, `lab.record_outcome`)

### PL-07: Forecast portfolios and agents
**Trigger:** `qrp models fport|agent --config <file>` · **Covers:** FPORT-01–FPORT-03, AGENT-01–AGENT-05
1. `lab` → `models`: `lab.benchmark`, `lab.project_capped`, `lab.check_weights`, `lab.training_window`
2. `models` → `lab`: `lab.Weights` into `lab.backtest`; outcomes into the ledger

### PL-08: Final verdict
**Trigger:** `qrp lab verdict` · **Covers:** VERD-01–VERD-03, WFWD-05
1. `lab` (ledger → verdict): `lab.entries`, `lab.score_lockbox`, `lab.benchmark` → `lab.Verdict`

## Project layout
```
packages/data/     → qrp_data (Python)
packages/lab/      → qrp_lab (Python)
packages/models/   → qrp_models (Python, torch)
packages/server/   → qrp_server (Python, FastAPI)
packages/app/      → TypeScript + React (Vite)
store/             → snapshot store, run and ledger databases (git-ignored, AD-06)
results/           → per-ledger-entry outputs and reports (git-ignored)
```

## Shared tech stack and tooling
| Area | Choice | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| Workspace | uv workspace, one lockfile; npm for `packages/app` | AD-01 |
| Language | Python 3.12; TypeScript 5.6 | AD-02 |
| Numerics | numpy 2.1, pandas 2.2, scipy 1.14, pandera 0.20 | AD-02 |
| Prices, actions, membership, identity | Sharadar SEP, ACTIONS, TICKERS, SP500 via `nasdaq-data-link` 1.0 | AD-03 |
| Risk-free rate | FRED DTB3 | AD-04 |
| Sector history | SEC EDGAR SIC codes, or Sharadar if PB-01 shows history | AD-05 |
| Trading calendar | `exchange_calendars` 4.13 (XNYS), cross-checked with SEP dates | AD-08 |
| Store | Parquet (pyarrow 17) + DuckDB 1.1; SQLite (WAL) for runs and ledger | AD-06, AD-07, AD-11 |
| Local API | FastAPI 0.115, uvicorn 0.32, pydantic 2.9, sse-starlette 2.1 | AD-09 |
| App | React 18.3, Vite 5.4, TanStack Query 5, React Router 6, Observable Plot 0.6 | AD-09 |
| Wire types | pydantic → JSON Schema → `json-schema-to-typescript` 15; ajv 8 in app tests | AD-09 |
| Optimisation | cvxpy 1.5 (Clarabel) | AD-14 |
| Neural nets | PyTorch 2.4 | AD-16 |
| RL | gymnasium 0.29, stable-baselines3 2.3 | AD-17 |
| Lint, types, tests | ruff, mypy, pytest, hypothesis; eslint, tsc, vitest; import-linter | AD-01 |

Packages are versioned together; one release tag for the repo.

## Cross-cutting concerns
- **Reproducibility (QR-07, PANEL-05):** every computation takes an explicit `SnapshotId`; every ledger entry records snapshot, config hash, seed and git SHA; CPU code is deterministic given those (no unseeded randomness, stable sorts).
- **Point-in-time discipline (PANEL-01, WFWD-04):** `data` filters by known-date; `lab.guard` checks every fit input against its `lab.Window` and raises `lab.LookAheadError`.
- **Claims (LEDG-05):** every comparison, ranking or claim is produced inside a started ledger entry and written under `results/<entry>/`; nothing else reports results (AD-11).
- **Skills:** `data-analysis` runs the quality report (VALID-07) and the characterisation steps (FACT-03, COV-02, FEAT-04); `null-hypothesis-testing` runs every ledger test (AD-11); `dataviz` and `frontend-design` govern the app (EXPL-06). Each component that runs a step names it.
- **Errors:** typed exceptions per contract; runs record failures per stock and carry on (INGST edge cases); CLI exits non-zero on a blocking failure.
- **Data costs (QR-01):** paid sources: Sharadar (existing subscription, $0 extra). FRED, SEC EDGAR, `exchange_calendars`: free. Extra monthly cost: $0.
- **Data locality (QR-04, QR-05):** the server binds 127.0.0.1 and checks the `Host` header; the only outbound vendor-data transfer is the GPU training bundle (AD-18).
- **Performance (QR-03, QR-08):** update downloads fetch by `lastupdated` (PB-01); promotion builds per-snapshot read views sorted by stock; run timings are stored in run records.
- **Configuration:** one `qrp.toml` (store path, API keys by env var name, server port); research settings live in each ledger registration.

## Decisions
### AD-01: Multi-package uv workspace with a separate TypeScript app
**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** system
- **Context:** The brief fixes Python for analysis and TypeScript/React for the app (two runtimes, QR-06). S4 brings torch, gymnasium and stable-baselines3 and a rented GPU (QR-02, QR-05), a stack the data and S2–S3 code should not carry. S1–S3 together would exceed ~20 modules, past the ~12-module grow trigger.
- **Decision:** Five packages: `data`, `lab`, `models`, `server` (Python, one uv workspace and lockfile) and `app` (npm). Dependency rules above, enforced by import-linter and a forbidden-import check in CI and the pre-commit hook.
- **Alternatives:** One Python package plus the app — mixes torch into S1–S3 installs and blurs the "only `data` writes the store" rule. Separate repos — version skew between the server's wire schemas and the app, for one person.
- **Consequences:** A component that grows out of its package moves by package, not by repo. Each package has its own architecture and contract docs. The GPU host installs `models` with its dependencies but receives only the training bundle (AD-18).

### AD-02: Python 3.12 numerics stack and TypeScript 5.6
**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** system
- **Context:** The brief requires Python for analysis and TypeScript/React for the app. Panels are dates × ~1,200 stocks over ~28 years: small enough for in-memory pandas (QR-03).
- **Decision:** Python 3.12 with numpy 2, pandas 2.2 (dates × `StockId` frames, NaN for missing per PANEL-04), scipy, pandera schemas for every frame that crosses a step; TypeScript 5.6 strict.
- **Alternatives:** polars — faster, but the reference code (RF-01–RF-03) and cvxpy/scikit-style libraries are pandas/numpy-shaped. xarray — adds a layer with no requirement needing it.
- **Consequences:** Frames carry NaN for missing values everywhere; no function may `fillna(0)` on returns (enforced by a lint rule and contract invariants).

### AD-03: Sharadar for prices, corporate actions, membership and identity
**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `data`
- **Context:** DATA-01–DATA-04 and UNIV-01–UNIV-03 need delisted stocks, membership with effective dates for ≥15 years, splits, dividends, spin-offs, ticker changes and delistings, and a permanent identifier; QR-01 caps extra spend at $50/month and the brief has a Sharadar subscription. Sharadar's [prices page](https://sharadar.com/prices) (read 2026-10-17) states SEP covers active and delisted US stocks with history to December 1997, an `sp500` table with "additions and deletions with effective dates" from January 1998, an `actions` table of splits, dividends, spin-offs, ticker changes and delisting details, `close` split-adjusted, `closeadj` adjusted for splits, dividends and spin-offs, and daily updates. The [fundamentals page](https://sharadar.com/fundamentals) (read 2026-10-17) states TICKERS has a permanent `permaticker` key and ACTIONS covers listing and delisting dates, delist reasons and acquisition counterparties. Neither page says whether delisting payouts carry a value, whether TICKERS keeps sector history, or how revisions show in `lastupdated`.
- **Decision:** Sharadar SEP (OHLCV, `closeunadj`), ACTIONS, TICKERS and SP500 through the Nasdaq Data Link tables API, behind the `data` `Source` protocol. `StockId` is the `permaticker`; each share class has its own (UNIV-03). Full downloads use the bulk export; updates filter on `lastupdated` (INGST-02).
- **Alternatives:** CRSP via WRDS — the reference standard for delisting returns, but needs an institutional licence. Norgate Data — has historical S&P membership and delisted stocks, but a second paid subscription close to the cap and Windows-centred tooling. Free scraped constituents (Wikipedia, EODHD free tier) — accurate only back to about 2000 per those sources, and no delisted prices.
- **Consequences:** History starts 1998, so DATA-01's 15 years holds with ~13 to spare. The adapter is the only Sharadar-aware code; a switch touches `data.vendors` only. Delisting returns depend on what ACTIONS records (AD-08).
- **Probe:** PB-01 — on the user's subscription: do SEP/ACTIONS/SP500/TICKERS return delisted members back to 1998; do ACTIONS delisting and acquisition rows carry a payout value; does TICKERS keep sector history; do `lastupdated` filters return revisions; and how long does a bulk export take?

### AD-04: FRED DTB3 for the daily risk-free rate
**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `data`
- **Context:** PANEL-06 and DATA-06 need a daily short-term rate on every trading date; QR-01 limits cost. The [FRED DTB3 page](https://fred.stlouisfed.org/series/DTB3) (read 2026-10-17) describes the 3-Month Treasury Bill secondary market rate, discount basis, daily, from the Federal Reserve's H.15 release.
- **Decision:** Download DTB3 via the FRED CSV endpoint; convert the discount rate to a bond-equivalent yield and then to a daily simple rate over the next trading day; on an exchange trading day with no H.15 value (bond-market holidays) carry the prior value with `filled=True`.
- **Alternatives:** Kenneth French daily `RF` — derived from a monthly rate and published with a lag. Effective federal funds rate — an overnight bank rate rather than a T-bill investable rate.
- **Consequences:** Free (QR-01). Carried values are visible in the panel and the quality report.
- **Probe:** none — the FRED series page documents the series, frequency and source; history depth is checked by VALID-06's coverage check on every snapshot.

### AD-05: Point-in-time sectors from SEC EDGAR SIC codes
**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `data`
- **Context:** DATA-07 and FCST-03 need each stock's sector on each date. Sharadar's [fundamentals page](https://sharadar.com/fundamentals) (read 2026-10-17) lists sector, industry, SIC sector, SIC industry and Fama industry fields on TICKERS but says nothing about history, so it may be the current value only. The SEC's company filing metadata records an SIC code with each filer, and is free (QR-01).
- **Decision:** If PB-01 shows TICKERS keeps dated classifications, use them. Otherwise build sector intervals from the SIC code recorded on each company's EDGAR filings, mapped to SIC divisions, linked to `StockId` by the CIK on TICKERS; a stock's first known sector is backfilled to its first membership date and flagged `assumed`.
- **Alternatives:** GICS history from S&P — licensed, beyond the budget. Current Sharadar sector for all dates — fails DATA-07's reclassification check.
- **Consequences:** SIC divisions are coarser than GICS sectors (10 vs 11, different boundaries); only FCST-03 uses sectors, as a fixed attribute.
- **Probe:** PB-02 — does EDGAR expose a per-filing SIC history for delisted S&P 500 members since 1998, and can every member be linked by CIK?

### AD-06: Bitemporal Parquet store with immutable snapshot manifests
**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `data`
- **Context:** INGST-03–INGST-05, DATA-04, PANEL-01 and PANEL-05 need immutable snapshots, both values of a revision with the date each was known, safe resume, and reproducible pinned reads; QR-03 and QR-08 need fast reads on a laptop.
- **Decision:** Every record row carries `ref_date`, `known_date`, `known_basis` (`observed` when first seen by a run; `assumed_ref_date` for backfilled history, known at the close of its reference date) and `run_id`. Runs append new Parquet part files and never modify one. A snapshot is a manifest (`store/snapshots/<id>/manifest.json`) listing the part files it includes plus vendor watermarks; `store/CURRENT` names the promoted snapshot and is replaced by atomic rename. Promotion builds a derived, rebuildable read view per snapshot (latest version per key, sorted by stock and date). DuckDB queries the parts and views.
- **Alternatives:** A database (Postgres, DuckDB file) with update-in-place — revisions and immutability need hand-built versioning, and a failed run can corrupt the current state. Full copy per snapshot — simple, but ~1–2 GB per daily update.
- **Consequences:** Snapshots share part files, so daily updates add MBs. An as-of-T read takes, per key, the newest version with `known_date ≤ T`. Garbage collection of unreferenced parts is a later manual command.

### AD-07: Run records and live events in SQLite, pushed by the server over SSE
**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `data`, `server`
- **Context:** INGST-06 and RUNS-01–RUNS-04 need durable run records and ≤2 s live updates that survive the app being closed; the "process dies" edge case needs detection within 10 s. Runs start from the CLI, not the app.
- **Decision:** Run processes write the run row and an append-only event table (`seq`, time, kind, stock, counts, message) to `store/runs.sqlite` in WAL mode, plus a heartbeat every 2 s. `data.run_record` reports a `running` run with no heartbeat for 10 s as `stopped_unexpectedly`. The server polls events by `seq` every 0.5 s and streams them over SSE; a new subscriber first gets the run's state so far (RUNS-04).
- **Alternatives:** WebSocket from the run process to the server — couples the run to the server being up. Log-file tailing — no structured counts, harder to resume by `seq`.
- **Consequences:** No message broker; the run process never needs the server. Worst-case latency ≈ poll interval + SSE flush, well under 2 s.

### AD-08: Returns built from unadjusted prices, actions and the exchange calendar
**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `data`
- **Context:** VALID-05 needs adjusted returns equal to economic total return within 0.01 pp; PANEL-03 needs delisting returns (cash payout, or −100% flagged estimated); PANEL-04 forbids filled gaps; DATA-05 and VALID-03 need the exchange calendar. RF-03 counts horizons in rows, which spec FCST-01 forbids.
- **Decision:** Daily total return = (unadjusted close × split factor + cash dividend) / previous unadjusted close − 1, with spin-offs taken from Sharadar's `closeadj` ratio on the ex-date; VALID-05 cross-checks every action day against `closeadj`. Delisting return: the recorded payout or final price versus the last close; with neither, −100% and `estimated=True`. Trading days come from `exchange_calendars` XNYS; a date where SEP has bars for most members but XNYS says closed (or the reverse) fails validation.
- **Alternatives:** Use `closeadj` returns directly — no independent check of VALID-05 and no delisting payouts. A hand-maintained holiday list — error-prone for special closures.
- **Consequences:** Every return in the platform comes from `data.returns_panel`; horizons everywhere count XNYS sessions (AD-15).
- **Probe:** PB-01 — whether ACTIONS gives payouts determines how many delisting returns are `estimated`.

### AD-09: Local FastAPI server with wire schemas generated into the app
**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `server`, `app`
- **Context:** QR-04 (local-only), QR-06 (each shape declared once and checked on both sides), QR-08 (≤2 s stock page, ≤1 s membership) and RUNS-01 (≤2 s live updates).
- **Decision:** `server` is FastAPI on uvicorn bound to `127.0.0.1`, rejecting any non-loopback `Host` header. Every response body is a pydantic model in `qrp_server.wire`; `qrp server export-schema` writes JSON Schema to `packages/app/src/wire/schema.json`, and `json-schema-to-typescript` generates `wire.ts`. CI fails when the regenerated schema or `wire.ts` differs from the committed one; server tests validate responses against the models, and app tests validate fixtures with ajv. Live runs use SSE.
- **Alternatives:** Hand-written TS types — drift is invisible (fails QR-06). OpenAPI client generators — generate whole clients we don't need; the schema-only path is smaller.
- **Consequences:** Python is the single source of every wire shape; changing one field on one side fails the check (QR-06's test).

### AD-10: One walk-forward schedule with per-method gaps and a look-ahead guard
**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `lab`, `models`
- **Context:** WFWD-01–WFWD-06 fix 21-day test blocks after ≥756 days, a gap ≥ max(5, longest label horizon), fit-time look-ahead errors and a 504-day lockbox. FCST-02, FACT-01, DENOI-03 and AGENT-01 rely on the same windows.
- **Decision:** `lab.schedule(snapshot)` derives the test blocks once from the snapshot's calendar: the lockbox is the last 504 sessions, and test blocks tile the sessions from 757 to the lockbox start. A method gets its training window from `lab.training_window(period, horizon=…, length=…)`, which sets the gap to max(5, horizon) and ends training before it; length defaults to 756 sessions (rolling), and a registration may choose expanding. Every fit entry point passes its inputs through `lab.guard`, which raises `lab.LookAheadError` on any row dated after the window's cutoff, or any label whose horizon ends on or after the test start.
- **Alternatives:** A schedule per method — breaks WFWD-01. Trusting callers to slice correctly — WFWD-04 needs a failing run, not a convention.
- **Consequences:** Test periods are identical across methods by construction; only the training cutoffs differ by horizon. A period a method cannot fit is scored as a failure (WFWD edge case), never dropped.

### AD-11: SQLite hypothesis ledger, with tests run through the null-hypothesis-testing skill
**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `lab`, `models`
- **Context:** LEDG-01–LEDG-06 need frozen registrations, outcomes for failures too, families for correction, and no unregistered claims; WFWD-05 needs a once-only lockbox gate; QR-07 needs reproducible entries.
- **Decision:** `store/ledger.sqlite` holds registrations (with a content hash), runs, outcomes and lockbox scores; triggers forbid updating a registration once its run has started. Every comparison names its correction family at registration; the family's members (abandoned and incomplete ones included) are fixed by the registrations naming it. The test step of every entry is run with the `null-hypothesis-testing` skill: `lab.TestInput` carries the registered null, metric, per-period paired series, family and α; the skill chooses the null distribution and correction; the result is recorded as `lab.TestOutcome`. Results are written under `results/<entry>/` and are readable only through a started entry.
- **Alternatives:** Ledger as YAML files in git — easy to edit after the fact (fails LEDG-02). A pre-registration service (OSF) — external and manual for hundreds of entries.
- **Consequences:** Every package that makes a claim registers through `lab`'s ledger functions; the families are listed where each stage's component registers them.

### AD-12: Factors by PCA with a parallel-analysis count and per-date residual regression
**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `lab`
- **Context:** FACT-01–FACT-05: window-only fits, a noise-based factor count (0 kept in ≥95% of noise trials), ≥50% drop in mean absolute correlation, no zero-filling, and residuals as of any date. RF-01 shows the market-mode removal but fits on the full sample, zero-fills gaps and fixes k.
- **Decision:** Per training window, keep stocks with ≥60 observations, standardise with window statistics, estimate the pairwise-complete correlation matrix and project it to the nearest PSD matrix. Choose k by parallel analysis: eigenvalue i is kept if it exceeds the 95th percentile of the i-th eigenvalue over 100 column-permuted copies that keep each stock's missing pattern. Factor returns per date come from a cross-sectional regression of that date's available returns on the loadings; residuals are defined only where a return exists. `lab.residual_returns(T)` uses loadings from the window that ends before T.
- **Alternatives:** Marchenko–Pastur edge with σ² = 1 — miscalibrated with missing data and fat tails. Fixed k = 1 as in RF-01 — fails FACT-02.
- **Consequences:** FACT-02's 95% property is the parallel-analysis threshold by construction, and is checked on simulated noise in tests. The FACT-03 check runs with the `data-analysis` skill.

### AD-13: Denoising by constant residual eigenvalue at the fitted Marchenko–Pastur edge
**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `lab`
- **Context:** DENOI-01–DENOI-03: keep signal eigenvalues, replace noise eigenvalues by their average, keep the trace, return a valid PD correlation matrix, fit on the window only, including when T < N.
- **Decision:** Fit the Marchenko–Pastur σ² to the window's eigenvalue density by maximum likelihood (excluding eigenvalues above the parallel-analysis count of AD-12); eigenvalues below the fitted edge λ₊ = σ²(1+√(N/T))² are replaced by their mean. Rebuild, then rescale to a unit diagonal. DENOI-01 is checked on the eigenvalues before the rescale; DENOI-02 after.
- **Alternatives:** Targeted shrinkage of noise eigenvalues only (keeps more structure, but not what DENOI-01 specifies). Clipping at the edge without averaging — not trace-preserving.
- **Consequences:** The matrix stays PD whenever the noise eigenvalues' mean is positive, which holds while the trace is N; T/N is recorded per window (DENOI edge case).

### AD-14: Portfolio construction with cvxpy, ERC by Newton, HRP in-house
**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `lab`
- **Context:** PORT-01 (long-only, fully invested, ≤5%), PORT-06 (equal risk contributions within 1%), the infeasible-cap edge case, and COV-01's minimum-variance portfolio; FPORT-03 and AGENT-05 need the same constraints.
- **Decision:** Minimum variance: a QP in cvxpy with the Clarabel solver. Risk parity: Spinu's convex formulation solved by Newton's method, then checked against PORT-01 (with ~500 stocks the ERC weights sit far below 5%; a breach is reported, not silently clipped). HRP: López de Prado's single-linkage recursive bisection, then capped by iterative redistribution. `lab.project_capped` is a Euclidean projection onto the capped simplex for weights from `models`.
- **Alternatives:** riskfolio-lib or skfolio for all four — broad, but heavier dependencies and less direct control over the infeasibility errors the spec names. scipy SLSQP for minimum variance — slow and fragile at N≈500.
- **Consequences:** One `lab.check_weights` enforces PORT-01 for every method, FPORT and AGENT included.

### AD-15: Targets counted in exchange sessions
**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `lab`, `models`
- **Context:** FCST-01 needs horizons in trading days with a missing target when any day lacks a bar; SIG-01 needs the T+1..T+5 residual return; WFWD-03 and FCST-02 need each label's end date. RF-03 counts rows, silently drops each stock's last days and does not check prices.
- **Decision:** `lab.forward_targets` computes forward log returns, forward realised volatility and forward residual returns over horizons counted on the XNYS calendar; a target is NaN if any session in the horizon lacks a return; each target row carries its `label_end` date so `lab.guard` can enforce the gap. Delisting returns count as the final day's return.
- **Alternatives:** Adopt RF-03 as is — fails FCST-01 on halts.
- **Consequences:** S3 and S4 share one target definition; features never see `label_end` columns.

### AD-16: Plain PyTorch sequence forecasters
**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `models`
- **Context:** FCST-01–FCST-07: separate static, known-ahead and observed inputs; point-in-time static attributes with an "unknown" category; training-window normalisation; purged splits; reproducibility within 1e-6. RF-02's window builder has no purge, a full-sample vocabulary and non-PIT statics.
- **Decision:** Two models in plain PyTorch 2.4: a compact temporal fusion transformer (variable selection, LSTM encoder, attention) and an LSTM. A window builder derived from RF-02 with its caveats fixed: origin-date splits purged by `lab.guard`, vocabulary and normalisation fit per training window with an `unknown` index, statics read point-in-time from `data.sectors`. Deterministic algorithms on, seeds recorded, and a CPU smoke configuration.
- **Alternatives:** pytorch-forecasting — has a TFT, but its own normalisers, encoders and splits fight the walk-forward and the guard. Darts — same issue, plus more dependencies.
- **Consequences:** We own the model code (~a few hundred lines). Baselines (trailing 63-day vol, EWMA vol, zero return) run in the same harness.

### AD-17: PPO agents with stable-baselines3 in a training-window market
**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `models`
- **Context:** AGENT-01–AGENT-05: train only on training-window data, pay BTEST's costs, a 20-configuration budget per line, a stop rule and valid portfolio actions.
- **Decision:** A gymnasium environment replays training-window returns through `lab.backtest`'s cost model; actions are logits mapped through softmax and `lab.project_capped` onto the capped simplex. PPO from stable-baselines3. Each line's budget is a ledger registration; the 21st configuration and any run on a stopped line are refused by the ledger.
- **Alternatives:** Hand-written PPO — more code to get right for no requirement. RLlib — distributed machinery a laptop/single GPU doesn't need.
- **Consequences:** Divergent runs are recorded as failed and count against the budget (AGENT edge case).

### AD-18: GPU training bundles, shipped and wiped
**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `models`
- **Context:** QR-02 caps GPU spend at $100/month; QR-05 allows training copies on rented GPUs only if deleted at the end of the rental; the brief rents occasionally.
- **Decision:** `models` writes a training bundle (windowed, normalised arrays, config, seeds; no raw vendor tables) that is copied over SSH to any hourly GPU host, trained there with the same `models` code, and copied back as checkpoints and forecasts. A teardown command deletes the bundle and checkpoints on the host, verifies the deletion, and logs it with the rental's hours and cost to `store/gpu_log.sqlite`; `qrp models gpu-budget` refuses a new rental when the month's logged spend would pass $100.
- **Alternatives:** Cloud notebooks (Colab) — data persists in the user's drive, harder to prove deletion. A fixed cloud provider SDK — ties the design to one vendor for an occasional need.
- **Consequences:** The GPU vendor stays an open choice; any SSH-reachable host works. Spend tracking is from logged hours × rate, reconciled with invoices by hand (QR-02's check).

## Risks and open questions
- PB-01 (Sharadar coverage on the user's subscription) gates AD-03, AD-05 and AD-08; if delisting payouts are missing, many delisting returns are `estimated` −100%, biasing results downward. Hand-off to `api-probe`.
- PB-02 (EDGAR SIC history) gates AD-05; if neither source has history, DATA-07 needs a spec change. Hand-off to `api-probe`.
- UNIV-01's 495–510 count: Sharadar quarterly snapshots plus change rows may disagree around same-day add/remove pairs; VALID-06 surfaces it, and the UNIV-04 check set (≥30 announced changes) must be assembled by the researcher from S&P press releases.
- DENOI-01 vs DENOI-02: rescaling to a unit diagonal perturbs the kept eigenvalues slightly; AD-13 checks DENOI-01 before the rescale. Confirm that reading of the spec.
- PORT-06 with the 5% cap: if a cap ever binds, ERC within 1% and PORT-01 cannot both hold; the portfolio is reported as a failure for that period rather than silently clipped.
- QR-03's 6-hour full rebuild depends on the bulk export speed (PB-01).
- GPU rental vendor is not chosen; any SSH host fits AD-18.

Only `data` writes the snapshot store or run records; everything else reads through its contract.\n- `lab` may import `data`; never `models`, `server`.\n- `models` may import `lab` and `data`; it is the only package that imports `torch`, `gymnasium` or `stable_baselines3`.\n- `server` may import `data` only, and only `server` imports `fastapi`.\n- `app` imports no Python; it talks to `server` over HTTP through the generated wire types.\n- Enforced by `import-linter` contracts over the uv workspace and a forbidden-import check for the heavy stacks (AD-01).\n\n## Cross-package contracts\n- `data.membership_as_of`, `data.returns_panel`, `data.bars_panel`, `data.risk_free`, `data.sectors`, `data.Calendar` — provided by `data`, used by `lab`, `models`, `server`. [Contract](packages/data/contract.md)\n- `data.stock_history`, `data.find_stock`, `data.coverage`, `data.issues`, `data.list_runs`, `data.run_events` — provided by `data`, used by `server`. [Contract](packages/data/contract.md)\n- `server` wire types and routes (`server.MembershipBody` … `server.stream_run`) — provided by `server`, used by `app`. [Contract](packages/server/contract.md)\n- `lab.Schedule`, `lab.training_window`, `lab.guard`, ledger (`lab.register` … `lab.entries`), `lab.backtest`, `lab.check_weights`, `lab.project_capped`, `lab.forward_targets`, `lab.compute_features`, `lab.surviving_features`, `lab.benchmark` — provided by `lab`, used by `models`. [Contract](packages/lab/contract.md)\n- `models` publishes no symbol another package imports; its results reach `lab.write_verdict` only through ledger entries. [Contract](packages/models/contract.md)\n\n## System pipelines\n### PL-01: Data refresh\n**Trigger:** `qrp data download --full|--update [--resume RUN]` from the command line · **Covers:** INGST-01–INGST-06, VALID-01–VALID-08, RUNS-01–RUNS-04, UNIV-04\n1. `data` (ingest → store): `data.run_download` writes a staged `data.Snapshot` and run events\n2. `data` (validate): `data.validate_snapshot` quarantines, promotes or rejects; `data.ValidationResult`\n3. `data` → `server`: `data.list_runs`, `data.run_events` (polled from the run database)\n4. `server` → `app`: `server.stream_run` (SSE of `server.RunEventBody`), `server.list_runs`\n\n### PL-02: Explore the dataset\n**Trigger:** the researcher opens the app at `http://127.0.0.1:<port>` · **Covers:** EXPL-01–EXPL-06, QR-08\n1. `app` → `server`: `server.get_membership`, `server.search_stocks`, `server.get_stock`, `server.get_coverage`, `server.get_issues`\n2. `server` → `data`: `data.membership_as_of`, `data.find_stock`, `data.stock_history`, `data.coverage`, `data.issues` on the current snapshot\n\n### PL-03: Covariance estimator ranking\n**Trigger:** `qrp lab rank-covariance --entry-family COV` · **Covers:** WFWD-01–WFWD-06, FACT-01–FACT-05, DENOI-01–DENOI-03, COV-01–COV-04, BTEST-01–BTEST-04, LEDG-01–LEDG-05\n1. `data` → `lab`: `data.returns_panel`, `data.risk_free`, `data.membership_as_of` pinned to one snapshot\n2. `lab` (walkforward → covariance → portfolios → backtest → ledger): `lab.Schedule`, `lab.CovMatrix`, `lab.Weights`, `lab.BacktestResult`, `lab.TestOutcome`\n\n### PL-04: Classical portfolios and the S4 benchmark\n**Trigger:** `qrp lab classical` after PL-03's ranking is recorded · **Covers:** PORT-01–PORT-06, BTEST-01–BTEST-04, LEDG-01–LEDG-05\n1. `data` → `lab`: as PL-03 step 1\n2. `lab` (portfolios → backtest → ledger): `lab.Weights`, `lab.BacktestResult`; stores `lab.benchmark`\n\n### PL-05: Feature significance\n**Trigger:** `qrp lab features` after PL-04 · **Covers:** FEAT-01–FEAT-04, SIG-01–SIG-04, FACT-05\n1. `data` → `lab`: `data.bars_panel`, `data.returns_panel`\n2. `lab` (factors → targets → features → significance → ledger): `lab.FeaturePanel`, `lab.Targets`, `lab.TestOutcome`\n\n### PL-06: Forecaster training\n**Trigger:** `qrp models forecast --config <file>` on the laptop; the fit step on the GPU host · **Covers:** FCST-01–FCST-07, QR-02, QR-05\n1. `data` → `models`: `data.bars_panel`, `data.sectors`, `data.Calendar`\n2. `lab` → `models`: `lab.training_window`, `lab.guard`, `lab.forward_targets`, `lab.compute_features`, `lab.surviving_features`\n3. `models` → `lab`: ledger entries (`lab.register`, `lab.record_outcome`)\n\n### PL-07: Forecast portfolios and agents\n**Trigger:** `qrp models fport|agent --config <file>` · **Covers:** FPORT-01–FPORT-03, AGENT-01–AGENT-05\n1. `lab` → `models`: `lab.benchmark`, `lab.project_capped`, `lab.check_weights`, `lab.training_window`\n2. `models` → `lab`: `lab.Weights` into `lab.backtest`; outcomes into the ledger\n\n### PL-08: Final verdict\n**Trigger:** `qrp lab verdict` · **Covers:** VERD-01–VERD-03, WFWD-05\n1. `lab` (ledger → verdict): `lab.entries`, `lab.score_lockbox`, `lab.benchmark` → `lab.Verdict`\n\n## Project layout\n```\npackages/data/     → qrp_data (Python)\npackages/lab/      → qrp_lab (Python)\npackages/models/   → qrp_models (Python, torch)\npackages/server/   → qrp_server (Python, FastAPI)\npackages/app/      → TypeScript + React (Vite)\nstore/             → snapshot store, run and ledger databases (git-ignored, AD-06)\nresults/           → per-ledger-entry outputs and reports (git-ignored)\n```\n\n## Shared tech stack and tooling\n| Area | Choice | Decision |\n|---|---|---|\n| Workspace | uv workspace, one lockfile; npm for `packages/app` | AD-01 |\n| Language | Python 3.12; TypeScript 5.6 | AD-02 |\n| Numerics | numpy 2.1, pandas 2.2, scipy 1.14, pandera 0.20 | AD-02 |\n| Prices, actions, membership, identity | Sharadar SEP, ACTIONS, TICKERS, SP500 via `nasdaq-data-link` 1.0 | AD-03 |\n| Risk-free rate | FRED DTB3 | AD-04 |\n| Sector history | SEC EDGAR SIC codes, or Sharadar if PB-01 shows history | AD-05 |\n| Trading calendar | `exchange_calendars` 4.13 (XNYS), cross-checked with SEP dates | AD-08 |\n| Store | Parquet (pyarrow 17) + DuckDB 1.1; SQLite (WAL) for runs and ledger | AD-06, AD-07, AD-11 |\n| Local API | FastAPI 0.115, uvicorn 0.32, pydantic 2.9, sse-starlette 2.1 | AD-09 |\n| App | React 18.3, Vite 5.4, TanStack Query 5, React Router 6, Observable Plot 0.6 | AD-09 |\n| Wire types | pydantic → JSON Schema → `json-schema-to-typescript` 15; ajv 8 in app tests | AD-09 |\n| Optimisation | cvxpy 1.5 (Clarabel) | AD-14 |\n| Neural nets | PyTorch 2.4 | AD-16 |\n| RL | gymnasium 0.29, stable-baselines3 2.3 | AD-17 |\n| Lint, types, tests | ruff, mypy, pytest, hypothesis; eslint, tsc, vitest; import-linter | AD-01 |\n\nPackages are versioned together; one release tag for the repo.\n\n## Cross-cutting concerns\n- **Reproducibility (QR-07, PANEL-05):** every computation takes an explicit `SnapshotId`; every ledger entry records snapshot, config hash, seed and git SHA; CPU code is deterministic given those (no unseeded randomness, stable sorts).\n- **Point-in-time discipline (PANEL-01, WFWD-04):** `data` filters by known-date; `lab.guard` checks every fit input against its `lab.Window` and raises `lab.LookAheadError`.\n- **Claims (LEDG-05):** every comparison, ranking or claim is produced inside a started ledger entry and written under `results/<entry>/`; nothing else reports results (AD-11).\n- **Skills:** `data-analysis` runs the quality report (VALID-07) and the characterisation steps (FACT-03, COV-02, FEAT-04); `null-hypothesis-testing` runs every ledger test (AD-11); `dataviz` and `frontend-design` govern the app (EXPL-06). Each component that runs a step names it.\n- **Errors:** typed exceptions per contract; runs record failures per stock and carry on (INGST edge cases); CLI exits non-zero on a blocking failure.\n- **Data costs (QR-01):** paid sources: Sharadar (existing subscription, $0 extra). FRED, SEC EDGAR, `exchange_calendars`: free. Extra monthly cost: $0.\n- **Data locality (QR-04, QR-05):** the server binds 127.0.0.1 and checks the `Host` header; the only outbound vendor-data transfer is the GPU training bundle (AD-18).\n- **Performance (QR-03, QR-08):** update downloads fetch by `lastupdated` (PB-01); promotion builds per-snapshot read views sorted by stock; run timings are stored in run records.\n- **Configuration:** one `qrp.toml` (store path, API keys by env var name, server port); research settings live in each ledger registration.\n\n## Decisions\n### AD-01: Multi-package uv workspace with a separate TypeScript app\n**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** system\n- **Context:** The brief fixes Python for analysis and TypeScript/React for the app (two runtimes, QR-06). S4 brings torch, gymnasium and stable-baselines3 and a rented GPU (QR-02, QR-05), a stack the data and S2–S3 code should not carry. S1–S3 together would exceed ~20 modules, past the ~12-module grow trigger.\n- **Decision:** Five packages: `data`, `lab`, `models`, `server` (Python, one uv workspace and lockfile) and `app` (npm). Dependency rules above, enforced by import-linter and a forbidden-import check in CI and the pre-commit hook.\n- **Alternatives:** One Python package plus the app — mixes torch into S1–S3 installs and blurs the \"only `data` writes the store\" rule. Separate repos — version skew between the server's wire schemas and the app, for one person.\n- **Consequences:** A component that grows out of its package moves by package, not by repo. Each package has its own architecture and contract docs. The GPU host installs `models` with its dependencies but receives only the training bundle (AD-18).\n\n### AD-02: Python 3.12 numerics stack and TypeScript 5.6\n**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** system\n- **Context:** The brief requires Python for analysis and TypeScript/React for the app. Panels are dates × ~1,200 stocks over ~28 years: small enough for in-memory pandas (QR-03).\n- **Decision:** Python 3.12 with numpy 2, pandas 2.2 (dates × `StockId` frames, NaN for missing per PANEL-04), scipy, pandera schemas for every frame that crosses a step; TypeScript 5.6 strict.\n- **Alternatives:** polars — faster, but the reference code (RF-01–RF-03) and cvxpy/scikit-style libraries are pandas/numpy-shaped. xarray — adds a layer with no requirement needing it.\n- **Consequences:** Frames carry NaN for missing values everywhere; no function may `fillna(0)` on returns (enforced by a lint rule and contract invariants).\n\n### AD-03: Sharadar for prices, corporate actions, membership and identity\n**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `data`\n- **Context:** DATA-01–DATA-04 and UNIV-01–UNIV-03 need delisted stocks, membership with effective dates for ≥15 years, splits, dividends, spin-offs, ticker changes and delistings, and a permanent identifier; QR-01 caps extra spend at $50/month and the brief has a Sharadar subscription. Sharadar's [prices page](https://sharadar.com/prices) (read 2026-10-17) states SEP covers active and delisted US stocks with history to December 1997, an `sp500` table with \"additions and deletions with effective dates\" from January 1998, an `actions` table of splits, dividends, spin-offs, ticker changes and delisting details, `close` split-adjusted, `closeadj` adjusted for splits, dividends and spin-offs, and daily updates. The [fundamentals page](https://sharadar.com/fundamentals) (read 2026-10-17) states TICKERS has a permanent `permaticker` key and ACTIONS covers listing and delisting dates, delist reasons and acquisition counterparties. Neither page says whether delisting payouts carry a value, whether TICKERS keeps sector history, or how revisions show in `lastupdated`.\n- **Decision:** Sharadar SEP (OHLCV, `closeunadj`), ACTIONS, TICKERS and SP500 through the Nasdaq Data Link tables API, behind the `data` `Source` protocol. `StockId` is the `permaticker`; each share class has its own (UNIV-03). Full downloads use the bulk export; updates filter on `lastupdated` (INGST-02).\n- **Alternatives:** CRSP via WRDS — the reference standard for delisting returns, but needs an institutional licence. Norgate Data — has historical S&P membership and delisted stocks, but a second paid subscription close to the cap and Windows-centred tooling. Free scraped constituents (Wikipedia, EODHD free tier) — accurate only back to about 2000 per those sources, and no delisted prices.\n- **Consequences:** History starts 1998, so DATA-01's 15 years holds with ~13 to spare. The adapter is the only Sharadar-aware code; a switch touches `data.vendors` only. Delisting returns depend on what ACTIONS records (AD-08).\n- **Probe:** PB-01 — on the user's subscription: do SEP/ACTIONS/SP500/TICKERS return delisted members back to 1998; do ACTIONS delisting and acquisition rows carry a payout value; does TICKERS keep sector history; do `lastupdated` filters return revisions; and how long does a bulk export take?\n\n### AD-04: FRED DTB3 for the daily risk-free rate\n**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `data`\n- **Context:** PANEL-06 and DATA-06 need a daily short-term rate on every trading date; QR-01 limits cost. The [FRED DTB3 page](https://fred.stlouisfed.org/series/DTB3) (read 2026-10-17) describes the 3-Month Treasury Bill secondary market rate, discount basis, daily, from the Federal Reserve's H.15 release.\n- **Decision:** Download DTB3 via the FRED CSV endpoint; convert the discount rate to a bond-equivalent yield and then to a daily simple rate over the next trading day; on an exchange trading day with no H.15 value (bond-market holidays) carry the prior value with `filled=True`.\n- **Alternatives:** Kenneth French daily `RF` — derived from a monthly rate and published with a lag. Effective federal funds rate — an overnight bank rate rather than a T-bill investable rate.\n- **Consequences:** Free (QR-01). Carried values are visible in the panel and the quality report.\n- **Probe:** none — the FRED series page documents the series, frequency and source; history depth is checked by VALID-06's coverage check on every snapshot.\n\n### AD-05: Point-in-time sectors from SEC EDGAR SIC codes\n**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `data`\n- **Context:** DATA-07 and FCST-03 need each stock's sector on each date. Sharadar's [fundamentals page](https://sharadar.com/fundamentals) (read 2026-10-17) lists sector, industry, SIC sector, SIC industry and Fama industry fields on TICKERS but says nothing about history, so it may be the current value only. The SEC's company filing metadata records an SIC code with each filer, and is free (QR-01).\n- **Decision:** If PB-01 shows TICKERS keeps dated classifications, use them. Otherwise build sector intervals from the SIC code recorded on each company's EDGAR filings, mapped to SIC divisions, linked to `StockId` by the CIK on TICKERS; a stock's first known sector is backfilled to its first membership date and flagged `assumed`.\n- **Alternatives:** GICS history from S&P — licensed, beyond the budget. Current Sharadar sector for all dates — fails DATA-07's reclassification check.\n- **Consequences:** SIC divisions are coarser than GICS sectors (10 vs 11, different boundaries); only FCST-03 uses sectors, as a fixed attribute.\n- **Probe:** PB-02 — does EDGAR expose a per-filing SIC history for delisted S&P 500 members since 1998, and can every member be linked by CIK?\n\n### AD-06: Bitemporal Parquet store with immutable snapshot manifests\n**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `data`\n- **Context:** INGST-03–INGST-05, DATA-04, PANEL-01 and PANEL-05 need immutable snapshots, both values of a revision with the date each was known, safe resume, and reproducible pinned reads; QR-03 and QR-08 need fast reads on a laptop.\n- **Decision:** Every record row carries `ref_date`, `known_date`, `known_basis` (`observed` when first seen by a run; `assumed_ref_date` for backfilled history, known at the close of its reference date) and `run_id`. Runs append new Parquet part files and never modify one. A snapshot is a manifest (`store/snapshots/<id>/manifest.json`) listing the part files it includes plus vendor watermarks; `store/CURRENT` names the promoted snapshot and is replaced by atomic rename. Promotion builds a derived, rebuildable read view per snapshot (latest version per key, sorted by stock and date). DuckDB queries the parts and views.\n- **Alternatives:** A database (Postgres, DuckDB file) with update-in-place — revisions and immutability need hand-built versioning, and a failed run can corrupt the current state. Full copy per snapshot — simple, but ~1–2 GB per daily update.\n- **Consequences:** Snapshots share part files, so daily updates add MBs. An as-of-T read takes, per key, the newest version with `known_date ≤ T`. Garbage collection of unreferenced parts is a later manual command.\n\n### AD-07: Run records and live events in SQLite, pushed by the server over SSE\n**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `data`, `server`\n- **Context:** INGST-06 and RUNS-01–RUNS-04 need durable run records and ≤2 s live updates that survive the app being closed; the \"process dies\" edge case needs detection within 10 s. Runs start from the CLI, not the app.\n- **Decision:** Run processes write the run row and an append-only event table (`seq`, time, kind, stock, counts, message) to `store/runs.sqlite` in WAL mode, plus a heartbeat every 2 s. `data.run_record` reports a `running` run with no heartbeat for 10 s as `stopped_unexpectedly`. The server polls events by `seq` every 0.5 s and streams them over SSE; a new subscriber first gets the run's state so far (RUNS-04).\n- **Alternatives:** WebSocket from the run process to the server — couples the run to the server being up. Log-file tailing — no structured counts, harder to resume by `seq`.\n- **Consequences:** No message broker; the run process never needs the server. Worst-case latency ≈ poll interval + SSE flush, well under 2 s.\n\n### AD-08: Returns built from unadjusted prices, actions and the exchange calendar\n**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `data`\n- **Context:** VALID-05 needs adjusted returns equal to economic total return within 0.01 pp; PANEL-03 needs delisting returns (cash payout, or −100% flagged estimated); PANEL-04 forbids filled gaps; DATA-05 and VALID-03 need the exchange calendar. RF-03 counts horizons in rows, which spec FCST-01 forbids.\n- **Decision:** Daily total return = (unadjusted close × split factor + cash dividend) / previous unadjusted close − 1, with spin-offs taken from Sharadar's `closeadj` ratio on the ex-date; VALID-05 cross-checks every action day against `closeadj`. Delisting return: the recorded payout or final price versus the last close; with neither, −100% and `estimated=True`. Trading days come from `exchange_calendars` XNYS; a date where SEP has bars for most members but XNYS says closed (or the reverse) fails validation.\n- **Alternatives:** Use `closeadj` returns directly — no independent check of VALID-05 and no delisting payouts. A hand-maintained holiday list — error-prone for special closures.\n- **Consequences:** Every return in the platform comes from `data.returns_panel`; horizons everywhere count XNYS sessions (AD-15).\n- **Probe:** PB-01 — whether ACTIONS gives payouts determines how many delisting returns are `estimated`.\n\n### AD-09: Local FastAPI server with wire schemas generated into the app\n**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `server`, `app`\n- **Context:** QR-04 (local-only), QR-06 (each shape declared once and checked on both sides), QR-08 (≤2 s stock page, ≤1 s membership) and RUNS-01 (≤2 s live updates).\n- **Decision:** `server` is FastAPI on uvicorn bound to `127.0.0.1`, rejecting any non-loopback `Host` header. Every response body is a pydantic model in `qrp_server.wire`; `qrp server export-schema` writes JSON Schema to `packages/app/src/wire/schema.json`, and `json-schema-to-typescript` generates `wire.ts`. CI fails when the regenerated schema or `wire.ts` differs from the committed one; server tests validate responses against the models, and app tests validate fixtures with ajv. Live runs use SSE.\n- **Alternatives:** Hand-written TS types — drift is invisible (fails QR-06). OpenAPI client generators — generate whole clients we don't need; the schema-only path is smaller.\n- **Consequences:** Python is the single source of every wire shape; changing one field on one side fails the check (QR-06's test).\n\n### AD-10: One walk-forward schedule with per-method gaps and a look-ahead guard\n**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `lab`, `models`\n- **Context:** WFWD-01–WFWD-06 fix 21-day test blocks after ≥756 days, a gap ≥ max(5, longest label horizon), fit-time look-ahead errors and a 504-day lockbox. FCST-02, FACT-01, DENOI-03 and AGENT-01 rely on the same windows.\n- **Decision:** `lab.schedule(snapshot)` derives the test blocks once from the snapshot's calendar: the lockbox is the last 504 sessions, and test blocks tile the sessions from 757 to the lockbox start. A method gets its training window from `lab.training_window(period, horizon=…, length=…)`, which sets the gap to max(5, horizon) and ends training before it; length defaults to 756 sessions (rolling), and a registration may choose expanding. Every fit entry point passes its inputs through `lab.guard`, which raises `lab.LookAheadError` on any row dated after the window's cutoff, or any label whose horizon ends on or after the test start.\n- **Alternatives:** A schedule per method — breaks WFWD-01. Trusting callers to slice correctly — WFWD-04 needs a failing run, not a convention.\n- **Consequences:** Test periods are identical across methods by construction; only the training cutoffs differ by horizon. A period a method cannot fit is scored as a failure (WFWD edge case), never dropped.\n\n### AD-11: SQLite hypothesis ledger, with tests run through the null-hypothesis-testing skill\n**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `lab`, `models`\n- **Context:** LEDG-01–LEDG-06 need frozen registrations, outcomes for failures too, families for correction, and no unregistered claims; WFWD-05 needs a once-only lockbox gate; QR-07 needs reproducible entries.\n- **Decision:** `store/ledger.sqlite` holds registrations (with a content hash), runs, outcomes and lockbox scores; triggers forbid updating a registration once its run has started. Every comparison names its correction family at registration; the family's members (abandoned and incomplete ones included) are fixed by the registrations naming it. The test step of every entry is run with the `null-hypothesis-testing` skill: `lab.TestInput` carries the registered null, metric, per-period paired series, family and α; the skill chooses the null distribution and correction; the result is recorded as `lab.TestOutcome`. Results are written under `results/<entry>/` and are readable only through a started entry.\n- **Alternatives:** Ledger as YAML files in git — easy to edit after the fact (fails LEDG-02). A pre-registration service (OSF) — external and manual for hundreds of entries.\n- **Consequences:** Every package that makes a claim registers through `lab`'s ledger functions; the families are listed where each stage's component registers them.\n\n### AD-12: Factors by PCA with a parallel-analysis count and per-date residual regression\n**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `lab`\n- **Context:** FACT-01–FACT-05: window-only fits, a noise-based factor count (0 kept in ≥95% of noise trials), ≥50% drop in mean absolute correlation, no zero-filling, and residuals as of any date. RF-01 shows the market-mode removal but fits on the full sample, zero-fills gaps and fixes k.\n- **Decision:** Per training window, keep stocks with ≥60 observations, standardise with window statistics, estimate the pairwise-complete correlation matrix and project it to the nearest PSD matrix. Choose k by parallel analysis: eigenvalue i is kept if it exceeds the 95th percentile of the i-th eigenvalue over 100 column-permuted copies that keep each stock's missing pattern. Factor returns per date come from a cross-sectional regression of that date's available returns on the loadings; residuals are defined only where a return exists. `lab.residual_returns(T)` uses loadings from the window that ends before T.\n- **Alternatives:** Marchenko–Pastur edge with σ² = 1 — miscalibrated with missing data and fat tails. Fixed k = 1 as in RF-01 — fails FACT-02.\n- **Consequences:** FACT-02's 95% property is the parallel-analysis threshold by construction, and is checked on simulated noise in tests. The FACT-03 check runs with the `data-analysis` skill.\n\n### AD-13: Denoising by constant residual eigenvalue at the fitted Marchenko–Pastur edge\n**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `lab`\n- **Context:** DENOI-01–DENOI-03: keep signal eigenvalues, replace noise eigenvalues by their average, keep the trace, return a valid PD correlation matrix, fit on the window only, including when T < N.\n- **Decision:** Fit the Marchenko–Pastur σ² to the window's eigenvalue density by maximum likelihood (excluding eigenvalues above the parallel-analysis count of AD-12); eigenvalues below the fitted edge λ₊ = σ²(1+√(N/T))² are replaced by their mean. Rebuild, then rescale to a unit diagonal. DENOI-01 is checked on the eigenvalues before the rescale; DENOI-02 after.\n- **Alternatives:** Targeted shrinkage of noise eigenvalues only (keeps more structure, but not what DENOI-01 specifies). Clipping at the edge without averaging — not trace-preserving.\n- **Consequences:** The matrix stays PD whenever the noise eigenvalues' mean is positive, which holds while the trace is N; T/N is recorded per window (DENOI edge case).\n\n### AD-14: Portfolio construction with cvxpy, ERC by Newton, HRP in-house\n**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `lab`\n- **Context:** PORT-01 (long-only, fully invested, ≤5%), PORT-06 (equal risk contributions within 1%), the infeasible-cap edge case, and COV-01's minimum-variance portfolio; FPORT-03 and AGENT-05 need the same constraints.\n- **Decision:** Minimum variance: a QP in cvxpy with the Clarabel solver. Risk parity: Spinu's convex formulation solved by Newton's method, then checked against PORT-01 (with ~500 stocks the ERC weights sit far below 5%; a breach is reported, not silently clipped). HRP: López de Prado's single-linkage recursive bisection, then capped by iterative redistribution. `lab.project_capped` is a Euclidean projection onto the capped simplex for weights from `models`.\n- **Alternatives:** riskfolio-lib or skfolio for all four — broad, but heavier dependencies and less direct control over the infeasibility errors the spec names. scipy SLSQP for minimum variance — slow and fragile at N≈500.\n- **Consequences:** One `lab.check_weights` enforces PORT-01 for every method, FPORT and AGENT included.\n\n### AD-15: Targets counted in exchange sessions\n**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `lab`, `models`\n- **Context:** FCST-01 needs horizons in trading days with a missing target when any day lacks a bar; SIG-01 needs the T+1..T+5 residual return; WFWD-03 and FCST-02 need each label's end date. RF-03 counts rows, silently drops each stock's last days and does not check prices.\n- **Decision:** `lab.forward_targets` computes forward log returns, forward realised volatility and forward residual returns over horizons counted on the XNYS calendar; a target is NaN if any session in the horizon lacks a return; each target row carries its `label_end` date so `lab.guard` can enforce the gap. Delisting returns count as the final day's return.\n- **Alternatives:** Adopt RF-03 as is — fails FCST-01 on halts.\n- **Consequences:** S3 and S4 share one target definition; features never see `label_end` columns.\n\n### AD-16: Plain PyTorch sequence forecasters\n**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `models`\n- **Context:** FCST-01–FCST-07: separate static, known-ahead and observed inputs; point-in-time static attributes with an \"unknown\" category; training-window normalisation; purged splits; reproducibility within 1e-6. RF-02's window builder has no purge, a full-sample vocabulary and non-PIT statics.\n- **Decision:** Two models in plain PyTorch 2.4: a compact temporal fusion transformer (variable selection, LSTM encoder, attention) and an LSTM. A window builder derived from RF-02 with its caveats fixed: origin-date splits purged by `lab.guard`, vocabulary and normalisation fit per training window with an `unknown` index, statics read point-in-time from `data.sectors`. Deterministic algorithms on, seeds recorded, and a CPU smoke configuration.\n- **Alternatives:** pytorch-forecasting — has a TFT, but its own normalisers, encoders and splits fight the walk-forward and the guard. Darts — same issue, plus more dependencies.\n- **Consequences:** We own the model code (~a few hundred lines). Baselines (trailing 63-day vol, EWMA vol, zero return) run in the same harness.\n\n### AD-17: PPO agents with stable-baselines3 in a training-window market\n**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `models`\n- **Context:** AGENT-01–AGENT-05: train only on training-window data, pay BTEST's costs, a 20-configuration budget per line, a stop rule and valid portfolio actions.\n- **Decision:** A gymnasium environment replays training-window returns through `lab.backtest`'s cost model; actions are logits mapped through softmax and `lab.project_capped` onto the capped simplex. PPO from stable-baselines3. Each line's budget is a ledger registration; the 21st configuration and any run on a stopped line are refused by the ledger.\n- **Alternatives:** Hand-written PPO — more code to get right for no requirement. RLlib — distributed machinery a laptop/single GPU doesn't need.\n- **Consequences:** Divergent runs are recorded as failed and count against the budget (AGENT edge case).\n\n### AD-18: GPU training bundles, shipped and wiped\n**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `models`\n- **Context:** QR-02 caps GPU spend at $100/month; QR-05 allows training copies on rented GPUs only if deleted at the end of the rental; the brief rents occasionally.\n- **Decision:** `models` writes a training bundle (windowed, normalised arrays, config, seeds; no raw vendor tables) that is copied over SSH to any hourly GPU host, trained there with the same `models` code, and copied back as checkpoints and forecasts. A teardown command deletes the bundle and checkpoints on the host, verifies the deletion, and logs it with the rental's hours and cost to `store/gpu_log.sqlite`; `qrp models gpu-budget` refuses a new rental when the month's logged spend would pass $100.\n- **Alternatives:** Cloud notebooks (Colab) — data persists in the user's drive, harder to prove deletion. A fixed cloud provider SDK — ties the design to one vendor for an occasional need.\n- **Consequences:** The GPU vendor stays an open choice; any SSH-reachable host works. Spend tracking is from logged hours × rate, reconciled with invoices by hand (QR-02's check).\n\n## Risks and open questions\n- PB-01 (Sharadar coverage on the user's subscription) gates AD-03, AD-05 and AD-08; if delisting payouts are missing, many delisting returns are `estimated` −100%, biasing results downward. Hand-off to `api-probe`.\n- PB-02 (EDGAR SIC history) gates AD-05; if neither source has history, DATA-07 needs a spec change. Hand-off to `api-probe`.\n- UNIV-01's 495–510 count: Sharadar quarterly snapshots plus change rows may disagree around same-day add/remove pairs; VALID-06 surfaces it, and the UNIV-04 check set (≥30 announced changes) must be assembled by the researcher from S&P press releases.\n- DENOI-01 vs DENOI-02: rescaling to a unit diagonal perturbs the kept eigenvalues slightly; AD-13 checks DENOI-01 before the rescale. Confirm that reading of the spec.\n- PORT-06 with the 5% cap: if a cap ever binds, ERC within 1% and PORT-01 cannot both hold; the portfolio is reported as a failure for that period rather than silently clipped.\n- QR-03's 6-hour full rebuild depends on the bulk export speed (PB-01).\n- GPU rental vendor is not chosen; any SSH host fits AD-18.\n\n## Changelog\n| Date | Change | Source | IDs |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 2026-10-17 | Initial architecture | spec.md | AD-01–AD-18 |\n"
}

# data — Architecture

**Package:** data · **Status:** Draft · **Last updated:** 2026-10-17 · **System:** [../../architecture.md](../../architecture.md)

## Overview
A CLI run downloads Sharadar, FRED and EDGAR data through `vendors`, appends bitemporal part files to `store` and writes a staged snapshot, recording progress in `runs`. `validate` checks the staged snapshot, quarantines bad records and promotes it only if nothing blocks. `universe` and `panel` answer point-in-time questions against one pinned snapshot; `calendar` supplies exchange sessions to all of them.

## Components
| Component | Responsibility | Covers | Depends on |
|---|---|---|---|
| `calendar` | XNYS sessions and session arithmetic for the covered history | DATA-05 | — |
| `vendors` | `Source` adapters for Sharadar (SEP, ACTIONS, TICKERS, SP500), FRED DTB3 and EDGAR SIC; retries with backoff (5 per request); credential check before any fetch | DATA-01–DATA-03, DATA-06, DATA-07, QR-01, INGST edge cases | — |
| `store` | Append-only Parquet parts with `ref_date`/`known_date`/`known_basis`, snapshot manifests, atomic `CURRENT`, per-snapshot read views | INGST-03, INGST-04, DATA-04, PANEL-05 | `calendar` |
| `runs` | Run records, append-only events and heartbeats in `runs.sqlite`; derived `stopped_unexpectedly` status | INGST-06, RUNS-01–RUNS-04 (producer side) | — |
| `ingest` | Full and update downloads over every stock ever a member, per-stock resume, staged snapshot; timings for QR-03 | INGST-01, INGST-02, INGST-05, INGST-06, QR-03 | `vendors`, `store`, `runs`, `calendar` |
| `validate` | Price, gap, large-move, corporate-action, membership and duplicate checks; quarantine; overrides; promotion; revalidation. Its quality-report step runs with the `data-analysis` skill | VALID-01–VALID-08 | `store`, `runs`, `calendar`, `universe`, `panel` |
| `universe` | Permanent identity (`permaticker`), ticker history, membership intervals and as-of membership | UNIV-01–UNIV-04 | `store`, `calendar` |
| `panel` | Point-in-time returns, bars, risk-free and sector panels; delisting returns; coverage | PANEL-01–PANEL-06, DATA-07 | `store`, `universe`, `calendar` |

Validation reads through `universe` and `panel` on the *staged* snapshot (they accept any snapshot id, not only promoted ones), so the checks see exactly what consumers would.

## Interfaces
See [contract.md](contract.md).

## Data
- **Sources:** Sharadar SEP/ACTIONS/TICKERS/SP500 (AD-03; DATA-01–DATA-04), FRED DTB3 (AD-04; DATA-06), EDGAR SIC or Sharadar sectors (AD-05; DATA-07), `exchange_calendars` XNYS (AD-08; DATA-05).
- **Store layout (AD-06):**
  ```
  store/parts/<table>/<run_id>-<n>.parquet     immutable rows: key, ref_date, known_date, known_basis, run_id, values
  store/snapshots/<snapshot_id>/manifest.json  parts included, vendor watermarks, status, validation id
  store/snapshots/<snapshot_id>/view/*.parquet latest version per key, sorted by (stock, date); rebuildable
  store/snapshots/<snapshot_id>/validation/    issues.parquet, result.json, quality report
  store/overrides.parquet                      researcher overrides (stock, date, reason, date recorded)
  store/CURRENT                                promoted snapshot id (atomic rename)
  store/runs.sqlite                            runs, events, heartbeats (AD-07)
  ```
- **Universe scope:** every `permaticker` that appears in SP500 additions, removals or snapshots since 1998; bars from 252 sessions before first membership (DATA-02).
- **Known dates:** backfilled rows are `assumed_ref_date`; rows first seen by an update, and every revision, are `observed` at the run's date (INGST-04, PANEL-01).
- **Returns (AD-08):** total return from unadjusted close, split factor and cash dividends; spin-offs via `closeadj`; delisting payout or −100% `estimated`.

## Cross-cutting concerns
- **Errors:** `CoverageError` (outside history, names the range), `SnapshotError` (unknown, unpromoted when one is required, or mixed), `CredentialsError` (before any fetch), `VendorError` per stock (recorded, run continues).
- **Performance:** read views keep `stock_history` and `membership_as_of` under the QR-08 budget; update runs fetch by `lastupdated` (QR-03).
- **Testing:** vendor adapters replay recorded responses; store and panel tests use synthetic snapshots with planted revisions, gaps, delistings and ticker reuse.

## Project layout
```
packages/data/src/qrp_data/<component>/   → one folder per component; __init__ exports its contract
packages/data/tests/                      → folders only; files are planned in pipelines.md
```
